# @nexusdi/codemod 0.4 Implementation Plan

> For agentic workers: REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

Goal: Build and publish `@nexusdi/codemod`, which rewrites a NexusDI 0.3 codebase to the 0.4 API as far as a mechanical rewrite can go and marks the rest with `// TODO(nexusdi-0.4): <reason> (<code>)`.

Architecture: A CLI (`npx @nexusdi/codemod 0.4 [paths...]`) loads the project with ts-morph, runs ten transforms in the fixed order of spec §13.2 over every file, then writes the files and a JSON or Markdown report, or prints a unified diff. Transforms read the code through ts-morph and record text edits against the original offsets; the runner applies each transform's edits to a file in one pass, so no transform ever calls ts-morph's manipulation API. Transforms recognise the 0.3 API by its imports from `@nexusdi/core`, so a project migrates whether `@nexusdi/core` resolves to 0.3.1, to a 0.4 workspace source, or to nothing.

Tech Stack: TypeScript 6.0.3 (workspace), ts-morph 28 (bundles TypeScript 6.0.2), jsonc-parser 3, diff 9, Node 22+ `util.parseArgs`, Vitest 4, Nx 23 (`@nx/js:library`, tsc build).

Spec: `specs/2026-09-23-core-0.4-design.md` on `spec/core-0.4` at `b5ab435`. This plan implements §13 and the codemod parts of §12, §14 and §17. It reads §3 and §4 for the 0.4 API the output targets.

## Global Constraints

- Package name `@nexusdi/codemod`, directory `libs/codemod`, Nx project name `@nexusdi/codemod`, commit scope `codemod` (spec §12, CONTRIBUTING "Scopes").
- CLI: `npx @nexusdi/codemod 0.4 [paths...]` with `--tsconfig <path>` (default `./tsconfig.json`), `--dry-run` (print a unified diff, write nothing), `--report <path>` (default `./nexusdi-0.4-report.json`; a path ending in `.md` writes Markdown), `--only <id,...>` (spec §13.2).
- Exit codes: `0` when the migration completes, TODOs included; `1` when a transform throws or the project fails to load (spec §13.2); `2` for a command-line error (this plan, see "Decisions").
- Transform order, verbatim: `symbol-tokens`, `services`, `property-injection`, `module-config`, `container-bootstrap`, `module-exports`, `await-get`, `container-leftovers`, `imports`, `tsconfig` (spec §13.2).
- TODO marker: `// TODO(nexusdi-0.4): <reason> (<code>)`. `TODO_CODES` holds exactly the 26 codes of spec §13.2 and `NOTE_CODES` holds `undecorated-param-injected`; `context.todo()` and `context.note()` accept only these; a code is never renamed or reused (spec §13.2 at `b5ab435`).
- The package exports `TODO_CODES` and `NOTE_CODES` from its `.` entry, because the docs site's guard enumerates them (spec §13.2).
- Report JSON shape: `{ version: 1, migration, codemodVersion, files: [{ path, transforms, todos: [{ line, column, code, reason }], notes: [{ line, column, code, message }] }], summary: { filesChanged, todos, notes, byCode } }` (spec §13.2).
- Dependencies: `ts-morph`, `jsonc-parser`, `diff`. Core's zero-dependency rule does not apply (spec §13.2).
- `engines.node` `>=22`, `"type": "module"`, nodenext ESM with explicit `.js` import extensions (spec §12).
- Fixtures live under `libs/codemod/src/migrations/0.4/__fixtures__/<transform-id>/<case>/` with `input.ts` (or `input/`), `output.ts` (or `output/`) and, when the case produces TODOs or notes, `report.json` (spec §13.2).
- Every fixture output typechecks against `@nexusdi/core` 0.4 through the `@nexusdi/source` condition, except inside statements that carry a TODO marker (this plan, see "Decisions").
- Published at `0.4.0-rc.0` on dist-tag `next` alongside core; the owner bootstraps the first publish by hand and configures the trusted publisher (spec §14).
- Test titles are in the indicative and start lower-case; one `describe` per export (`tools/repo-checks/src/test-title-mood.test.ts`, libraries repo convention).
- Error classes follow `libs/acl/src/errors.ts` in the libraries repo: a base class, `this.name` set in each constructor, readonly fields assigned in the constructor.
- Every commit message uses a scope from `commitlint.config.js` and ends with the line `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>`.

## Review Focus

1. A Vite 8 app runs the migrated `@Injectable` and `@Module` code through Oxc, which lowers only legacy decorators; the standard decorators reach the runtime as a syntax error. A user expects the migrated app to start. Task 25 pins it: the end-to-end test builds and tests the migrated React Router app with a decorator-lowering plugin, and "Decisions" names the owner decision this raises.
2. A container read from a framework context whose generated types are missing has type `any`, so the type-based container check fails. A user expects `container.get(TOKEN)` in a route loader to still get `TOKEN` exported from its module. Task 18 pins it with the `untyped-receiver` fixture.
3. A 0.3 project on TypeScript 5.x sets options TypeScript 6 deprecates (`moduleResolution: "node"`, `baseUrl`), and ts-morph 28 compiles with TypeScript 6.0.2. A user expects the project to load. Task 6 pins it with `loads a config that uses an option TypeScript 6 deprecates`.
4. A user fixes some TODOs and runs the codemod again. They expect nothing else to change and no TODO to repeat. Task 10 pins it: every fixture's output goes through the whole migration a second time and must come out unchanged with an empty report.
5. Test files often sit outside every tsconfig (the React Router example's `tests/`). A user who names them on the command line expects them migrated. Task 6 pins it with `restricts the run to the given paths, and adds a named file no config includes`.

## Decisions this plan makes where the spec is silent

- Fixture runs execute the whole registered pipeline. The spec's test-strategy bullet runs one transform per case, and a single transform's output still holds 0.3 constructs the other transforms own (an `@Service` next to a rewritten token), so it can never typecheck against 0.4, and spec §13.2 requires every "after" fixture to typecheck. The directory a case sits in names the transform whose behaviour it pins. `case.json` can still restrict a case with `transforms`.
- A statement under a TODO marker is exempt from the fixture typecheck. A TODO marks code the codemod could not migrate, and such code does not compile against 0.4 by definition (`container.resolve()`, `new Nexus()`).
- The docs and 0.3 test-file corpus (Task 23) is exempt from the typecheck. Its inputs do not compile against 0.3 either: docs snippets reference names they never declare. The corpus pins that the migration completes, matches its reviewed golden, and is idempotent.
- A command-line error exits `2`. Spec §13.2 defines `0` and `1` only; `2` is the POSIX usage-error convention, so a script can tell a typo in its own invocation from a migration that failed.
- Codes the spec list lacks a name for fold into the nearest listed code: a symbol used as a token and as a value → `untyped-token`; a class listed bare whose parameters have no deps → `undecorated-param`; a `DynamicModule` the codemod cannot convert → `dynamic-module-static`; `createModuleConfig(TOKEN, ...)` and `DynamicModule.forRoot()` without a name → `dynamic-module-config-api`; `new Nexus()` in a synchronous function or in an assignment → `top-level-container`; a conditional `set()`, an ambiguous provider, an export 0.4 rejects, a token its file does not export, and a module registered twice → `dynamic-module-graph`; a cross-module need on a module that takes options → `dynamic-module-unconfigured`; a leftover use of `Service`, `Provider` or `Optional` → `removed-metadata-api`; `DynamicModule` and `createModuleConfig` → `dynamic-module-config-api`.
- The state transforms share is `context.state` (typed `Migration04State`), which holds the spec's `moduleGraph` beside what `services` records for `module-config`. The context also gains `edits(file)`, `readTextFile`, `writeTextFile` and `todoInText`, which the edit model and the `tsconfig` transform need.
- A `Transform` has an optional `runProject(context)` next to `run(file, context)`. `tsconfig` edits JSON files and `module-exports` reads the whole graph, and neither is a per-file job.
- The module graph is built by `module-exports` from the sources as transforms 1 to 5 left them. Spec §13.2 says "built once from the 0.3 sources" and also "the tokens each provider depends on after transforms 2 and 4"; only the second reading has the `deps` those transforms write.
- `@Service(TOKEN) class C` and `{ token: T, useClass: C }` become `C` plus `provide(T, { useExisting: C })` for the token registered last, and `provide(T, { useClass: C, deps })` for any earlier token of the same class. 0.3.1 aliased `C` to the last token and built one instance per token, and this keeps both facts.
- A 0.3 `new Nexus()` at module level still gets its root module written; only the `new Nexus()` line itself keeps the `top-level-container` TODO.
- The `tsconfig` transform keeps comment lines above a removed flag and removes a comment on the flag's own line.
- The migration guide (`docs/docs/migration/0.3-to-0.4.md`, spec §13.1) is not in this plan: `docs/` does not exist on `chore/tooling-upgrade`, and the docs site has its own spec. The codemod README (Task 26) documents every code, and the guide links to it.
- The end-to-end test takes the 0.3 example from the parent of the commit that removed `@Service(` from `examples/react-ssr/app/modules/logger/logger.service.ts` (the engine plan's Task 36 port), found with `git log -S`. The tag `@nexusdi/core@0.3.1` holds the example before the tooling upgrade, whose `unstable_` React Router APIs the workspace's React Router 7.18 no longer exports. `NEXUSDI_E2E_REF` overrides the ref.

Owner decision needed before the first release: the spec has `services` write `@Injectable`, and Vite 8's Oxc does not lower standard decorators (the engine plan works around the same fact for core's own tests with `libs/core/vite.decorators.ts`). The plan follows the spec, and Task 25 adds a lowering plugin to the migrated example. The alternative is a decorator-free output (`provide(C, { deps })` in each module's `providers`, which is what the engine plan's hand port of the example does). Recommendation: keep the spec's output and add a `decorators-need-lowering` note code to the spec, emitted when the project has a `vite.config.*`, so the report tells Vite users what to add.

## Branches and task order

The codemod is built on `feat/codemod-0.4`, branched from `chore/tooling-upgrade`. Tasks 1 to 23 and 26 to 27 need nothing from the 0.4 engine: fixture inputs compile against the vendored 0.3.1 declarations in memory, and transforms only write text.

Two tasks need the engine. Rebase `feat/codemod-0.4` onto `feat/core-0.4` before each:

- Task 24 (fixture outputs typecheck against 0.4) needs the engine's public entry to declare, from `@nexusdi/core`: `Nexus` (with `static create`, `get`, `has`), `Token`, `InjectionToken`, `Provider`, `ProviderEntry`, `ModuleDefinition`, `provide`, `defineModule` with `with()` on a module defined with `options`, `optional`, `lazy`, `all`, `Injectable`, `Inject`, `Module`, `NexusError`, `MissingProviderError`, `NotVisibleError`, `InvalidTokenError`, `InvalidProviderError`, `InvalidModuleError`. Engine Tasks 3 to 8, 18 and 31.
- Task 25 (end-to-end) needs the engine's runtime: `Nexus.create` building singletons, module encapsulation and exports, `with()` options, `useExisting` aliases, `@Injectable` metadata read by the runtime, and `ProviderEntry` for the test helper. Engine Tasks 8, 10 to 18, 31 and 36 (the hand port, whose parent commit holds the 0.3 example).

## File structure

```
libs/codemod/
  package.json              bin nexusdi-codemod, exports ".", deps ts-morph, jsonc-parser, diff
  README.md                 usage, exit codes, every TODO and note code
  LICENSE
  eslint.config.mjs  tsconfig.json  tsconfig.lib.json  tsconfig.spec.json  vite.config.ts
  scripts/extract-corpus.mjs      writes the docs and 0.3 test-file corpus fixtures
  e2e/react-ssr.expected/         codemod output for the example, and its report
  e2e/react-ssr.resolved/         hand resolution of every file that has a TODO
  src/
    index.ts                public entry: TODO_CODES, NOTE_CODES and their types
    codes.ts                the code lists
    errors.ts               CodemodError and subclasses, with exit codes
    markers.ts              TODO and note markers, anchors, positions
    transform.ts            Transform, Migration, MigrationContext
    recorder.ts             pending edits, TODOs and notes per file; the context
    runner.ts               runMigration: transform-major loop, finalize
    core-api.ts             import-based @nexusdi/core detection, CoreTypes
    edit/text-edits.ts      TextEdits: edits against original offsets, planned imports
    edit/imports.ts         specifierFor, planImport
    edit/render-type.ts     renderType
    edit/jsonc.ts           JSONC read, remove, set, comment
    project/load-projects.ts   tsconfig discovery, references, paths
    report/report.ts        buildReport, renderMarkdown, renderSummary
    report/diff.ts          renderDiff
    main.ts                 the CLI as a function: main(argv, io) => exit code
    cli.ts                  the bin: #!/usr/bin/env node
    migrations/index.ts     MIGRATIONS keyed by version
    migrations/0.4/index.ts TRANSFORM_IDS and the ordered transforms
    migrations/0.4/state.ts Migration04State, legacyDecoratorsInUse
    migrations/0.4/token-sites.ts      classifyTokenSite
    migrations/0.4/modules.ts          module detection helpers
    migrations/0.4/provider-entries.ts ProvidersBuilder, renderArray, importKeys
    migrations/0.4/module-graph.ts     StaticModuleGraph
    migrations/0.4/transforms/<id>.ts  one file per transform
    migrations/0.4/__fixtures__/       fixture cases, and the corpus
    migrations/0.4/*.test.ts           fixtures, codes coverage, order, typecheck
    testing/harness.ts      in-memory fixture runner
    testing/vendor/core-0.3.1/         0.3.1 declarations from the npm tarball
    testing/vendor/core-async-branch/  declarations built from archive/async-container
    e2e/react-ssr.test.ts   the end-to-end test
```

## Phase 1: Package and infrastructure

### Task 1: Scaffold `libs/codemod` with the Nx generator and wire it into the repo

Files:

- Create (generator): `libs/codemod/package.json`, `tsconfig.json`, `tsconfig.lib.json`, `tsconfig.spec.json`, `eslint.config.mjs`, the test config, `README.md`, `src/index.ts`
- Create: `libs/codemod/LICENSE`, `libs/codemod/src/codes.ts`, `libs/codemod/src/codes.test.ts`, `libs/codemod/src/index.test.ts`
- Modify: `tsconfig.json` (the reference the generator adds), `commitlint.config.js`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `scripts/verify-packaging.mjs`, `.prettierignore`, `.fallowrc.jsonc`, `package-lock.json`
- Restore: `nx.json`, root `package.json`, `.verdaccio/` (generator collateral)

Interfaces:

- Consumes: nothing.
- Produces: `TODO_CODES: readonly TodoCode[]`, `NOTE_CODES: readonly NoteCode[]`, `type TodoCode`, `type NoteCode` in `src/codes.ts`, re-exported from `src/index.ts`. Every later task's `context.todo(node, code: TodoCode, reason)` is typed by these.

`libs/codemod/vite.config.ts`:

```ts
import { defineConfig } from 'vite';

export default defineConfig(() => ({
  root: import.meta.dirname,
  cacheDir: '../../node_modules/.vite/libs/codemod',
  // Vite's own tsconfig auto-discovery walks parent directories with no
  // boundary at the workspace root, which can escape a git worktree
  // entirely (see libs/core/vite.config.ts). Naming the file avoids that.
  tsconfig: './tsconfig.spec.json',
  test: {
    name: '@nexusdi/codemod',
    watch: false,
    globals: true,
    environment: 'node',
    include: ['src/**/*.{test,spec}.{js,mjs,cjs,ts,mts,cts,jsx,tsx}'],
    // A fixture file may be named like a test (`a.test.ts`); it is data, not a test.
    exclude: ['src/**/__fixtures__/**', 'node_modules/**'],
    reporters: ['default'],
    // ts-morph builds a program per fixture case; the end-to-end test sets its own, longer limits.
    testTimeout: 60_000,
    coverage: {
      reportsDirectory: './test-output/vitest/coverage',
      provider: 'v8' as const,
      exclude: ['src/**/__fixtures__/**', 'src/testing/vendor/**', 'src/cli.ts', 'src/index.ts'],
    },
  },
}));
```

`libs/codemod/eslint.config.mjs`:

```js
import baseConfig from '../../eslint.config.mjs';

export default [
  ...baseConfig,
  {
    // Fixture inputs are 0.3 code as users wrote it, fixture outputs are the
    // codemod's exact text, and the vendored declarations are copies of

    ignores: ['src/**/__fixtures__/**', 'src/testing/vendor/**', 'e2e/**'],

    files: ['**/*.json'],

            '{projectRoot}/eslint.config.{js,cjs,mjs,ts,cts,mts}',
            '{projectRoot}/vite.config.{js,ts,mjs,mts}',
            '{projectRoot}/scripts/**',

      ],
    },
    languageOptions: {
      parser: await import('jsonc-eslint-parser'),

];
```

- [ ] Step 8: Write the code lists and the entry

`libs/codemod/src/codes.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * Every code a TODO marker or a report note can carry, as section 13.2 of
 * the core 0.4 spec lists them.
 *
 * The codes are public API: the report prints them, the docs site has an
 * entry for each, and its guard enumerates these lists. Like the core error
 * codes, a code is never renamed or reused.
 */
export const TODO_CODES = [
  'untyped-token',
  'token-to-symbol',
  'undecorated-param',
  'provider-singleton-false',
  'provider-scope-option',
  'provider-type-option',
  'property-injection',
  'dynamic-module-typeof',
  'dynamic-module-static',
  'dynamic-module-unconfigured',
  'dynamic-module-spread',
  'dynamic-module-config-api',
  'top-level-container',
  'set-after-startup',
  'module-exports-cycle',
  'dynamic-module-graph',
  'container-resolve',
  'container-clear',
  'container-list',
  'child-container',
  'container-lifecycle',
  'removed-provider-type',
  'removed-metadata-api',
  'removed-guard',
  'error-message-match',
  'legacy-decorators-in-use',
] as const;
export type TodoCode = (typeof TODO_CODES)[number];

export const NOTE_CODES = ['undecorated-param-injected'] as const;
export type NoteCode = (typeof NOTE_CODES)[number];
```

- [ ] Step 3: Write the error classes

`libs/codemod/src/errors.ts`:

```ts
/** Base class for every error the codemod raises on purpose. */
export class CodemodError extends Error {
  /** The process exit code the CLI uses for this error. */
  readonly exitCode: 1 | 2;
  constructor(message: string, exitCode: 1 | 2, options?: { cause?: unknown }) {
    super(message, options);
    this.name = 'CodemodError';
    this.exitCode = exitCode;
  }
}

/**
 * The command line asks for something the codemod does not have.
 *
 * Exit code 2, the POSIX convention for a usage error, so a script can tell
 * a typo in its own invocation from a migration that failed.
 */
export class UsageError extends CodemodError {
  constructor(message: string) {
    super(message, 2);
    this.name = 'UsageError';
  }
}

/** The tsconfig or a file it names cannot be read. Nothing was changed. */
export class ProjectLoadError extends CodemodError {
  readonly tsconfig: string;
  constructor(tsconfig: string, detail: string) {
    super(`cannot load the project at ${tsconfig}: ${detail}`, 1);
    this.name = 'ProjectLoadError';
    this.tsconfig = tsconfig;
  }
}

/**
 * A transform threw on a file.
 *
 * The run stops and writes nothing, because the transforms after it read
 * its output. The transform id and the file are what a bug report needs.
 */
export class TransformError extends CodemodError {
  readonly transform: string;
  readonly file: string;
  constructor(transform: string, file: string, cause: unknown) {
    const detail = cause instanceof Error ? cause.message : String(cause);
    super(`transform ${transform} failed on ${file}: ${detail}`, 1, { cause });
    this.name = 'TransformError';
    this.transform = transform;
    this.file = file;
  }
}

/**
 * A TODO or note the run recorded is missing from the file it was recorded in.
 *
 * A later transform replaced the text that held it. The report would then
 * point at a line that says nothing, so the run stops instead.
 */
export class MarkerLostError extends CodemodError {
  readonly file: string;
  readonly code: string;
  constructor(file: string, code: string) {
    super(
      `the ${code} entry recorded for ${file} is no longer in the file: a later transform replaced the text that held it`,
      1,
    );
    this.name = 'MarkerLostError';
    this.file = file;
    this.code = code;
  }
}
```

The marker format carries the code in parentheses at the end of the line, so the runner can find each marker again after later transforms moved it, and a second run can see a statement is already marked.

```ts
  anchorOf,

  );

describe('todoMarker', () => {
  it('writes the prefix, the reason and the code', () => {

      '// TODO(nexusdi-0.4): list() is gone (container-list)',
    );

});

describe('anchorOf', () => {
  it('climbs from an expression to its statement', () => {
    const file = parse(
      'function f() {\n  const a = container.resolve(A);\n}\n',
    );
    const call = file.getFirstDescendantByKindOrThrow(
      SyntaxKind.CallExpression,

    expect(anchorOf(call).getText()).toBe('const a = container.resolve(A);');

    const file = parse(
      "const M = defineModule({\n  name: 'M',\n  imports: [Mod],\n});\n",

    const id = file
      .getDescendantsOfKind(SyntaxKind.Identifier)
      .find((i) => i.getText() === 'Mod');

    const literal = file.getFirstDescendantByKindOrThrow(
      SyntaxKind.StringLiteral,
    );
    expect(anchorOf(literal).getKind()).toBe(SyntaxKind.IfStatement);
  });
});

    const text = '// one\n// two\nconst a = 1;\n';
```

- [ ] Step 3: Write the markers module

`libs/codemod/src/markers.ts`:

```ts
import { Node, ts } from 'ts-morph';

import type { TodoCode } from './codes.js';

/** The prefix of every TODO marker. The migration guide tells users to search for it. */
export const TODO_PREFIX = '// TODO(nexusdi-0.4):';

/** `text` on one line: a reason that quotes multi-line code would otherwise break its marker. */
export function oneLine(text: string): string {
  return text.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
}

/** The marker line for one TODO, without indentation. */
export function todoMarker(code: TodoCode, reason: string): string {
  return `${TODO_PREFIX} ${oneLine(reason)} (${code})`;
}

/** A statement that is the unbraced body of an `if`, `else` or loop, which a marker line must not split from its head. */
function isUnbracedBody(node: Node): boolean {
  const parent = node.getParent();
  return (
    parent !== undefined &&
    (Node.isIfStatement(parent) ||
      Node.isForStatement(parent) ||
      Node.isForOfStatement(parent) ||
      Node.isForInStatement(parent) ||
      Node.isWhileStatement(parent) ||
      Node.isDoStatement(parent))
  );
}

/** Whether `node` is the first thing on its line. */
function startsLine(node: Node): boolean {
  const text = node.getSourceFile().getFullText();
  const start = node.getStart(true);
  return text.slice(text.lastIndexOf('\n', start - 1) + 1, start).trim() === '';
}

/**
 * The node a TODO marker goes above: the nearest statement, class member or
 * object property that contains `node` and starts its own line. A marker
 * inside an expression would break the line it sits on, so the search
 * climbs to the first node that owns whole lines.
 */
export function anchorOf(node: Node): Node {
  for (
    let current: Node | undefined = node;
    current;
    current = current.getParent()
  ) {
    if (Node.isSourceFile(current)) break;
    if (Node.isBlock(current) || isUnbracedBody(current)) continue;
    const owner =
      Node.isStatement(current) ||
      ts.isClassElement(current.compilerNode) ||
      Node.isPropertyAssignment(current) ||
      Node.isShorthandPropertyAssignment(current);
    if (owner && startsLine(current)) return current;
  }
  return node;
}

/** The start of the line `pos` is on. */
export function lineStartOf(text: string, pos: number): number {
  return text.lastIndexOf('\n', pos - 1) + 1;
}

/** The comment lines directly above the line `pos` is on, trimmed, nearest first. */
export function commentLinesAbove(text: string, pos: number): string[] {
  const lines: string[] = [];
  let end = lineStartOf(text, pos) - 1;
  while (end > 0) {
    const start = lineStartOf(text, end);
    const line = text.slice(start, end).trim();
    if (!line.startsWith('//')) break;
    lines.push(line);
    end = start - 1;
  }
  return lines;
}

/** Whether `node`'s anchor already carries a TODO marker with `code`. A transform leaves such a node as it is. */
export function hasMarker(node: Node, code: TodoCode): boolean {
  const text = node.getSourceFile().getFullText();
  return commentLinesAbove(text, anchorOf(node).getStart(true)).some(
    (line) => line.startsWith(TODO_PREFIX) && line.endsWith(`(${code})`),
  );
}

/** The placeholder `note()` leaves in the text until the run ends. */
export function noteMarker(id: number): string {
  return `/*nexusdi-note:${id}*/`;
}

const NOTE_MARKER = /\/\*nexusdi-note:(\d+)\*\//g;

/** A 1-based line and column. */
export interface Position {
  readonly line: number;
  readonly column: number;
}

/** The 1-based line and column of offset `pos` in `text`. */
export function positionOf(text: string, pos: number): Position {
  const lineStart = lineStartOf(text, pos);
  return {
    line: text.slice(0, lineStart).split('\n').length,
    column: pos - lineStart + 1,
  };
}

/** Removes every note placeholder, and returns where each one was in the text without them. */
export function stripNoteMarkers(text: string): {
  text: string;
  positions: Map<number, Position>;
} {
  const positions = new Map<number, Position>();
  let out = '';
  let last = 0;
  for (const match of text.matchAll(NOTE_MARKER)) {
    out += text.slice(last, match.index);
    positions.set(Number(match[1]), positionOf(out, out.length));
    last = match.index + match[0].length;
  }
  return { text: out + text.slice(last), positions };
}

/**
 * The position of the code under each marker line equal to `marker`, in
 * order of appearance. Stacked markers all point at the first line below
 * them that is not a marker.
 */
export function findTodoMarkers(text: string, marker: string): Position[] {
  const found: Position[] = [];
  const lines = text.split('\n');
  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
    if ((lines[i] ?? '').trim() !== marker) continue;
    let target = i + 1;
    while (
      target < lines.length &&
      (lines[target] ?? '').trim().startsWith(TODO_PREFIX)
    )
      target++;
    const content = lines[target] ?? '';
    found.push({
      line: target + 1,
      column: content.length - content.trimStart().length + 1,
    });
  }
  return found;
}
```

### Task 4: Text edits applied in one pass

Files:

- Create: `libs/codemod/src/edit/text-edits.ts`, `libs/codemod/src/edit/text-edits.test.ts`

Interfaces:

- Consumes: nothing from earlier tasks.
- Produces: `class TextEdits` with `readonly file: SourceFile`, `size`, `replaceRange(start, end, text)`, `replace(node, text)`, `insert(pos, text)`, `insertFirst(pos, text)`, `removeStatement(node)`, `plansImport(specifier, name)`, `addImport(specifier, name, typeOnly?)`, `textOf(node): string`, `dropWithin(node)`, `render(): string`; `OverlappingEditError`; `applyTo(text, edits, file)`; `indentOf(node): string`; `reindent(text, from, to): string`.

ts-morph's manipulation methods re-indent multi-line text by their own rules and forget every node they touch; a prototype of this codemod produced providers arrays indented six spaces too deep through `replaceWithText`. So transforms read through ts-morph and write through this class, and the runner applies each transform's edits to a file once.

```ts
import { Project, SyntaxKind } from 'ts-morph';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';

import {
  indentOf,
  OverlappingEditError,
  reindent,
  TextEdits,
} from './text-edits.js';

function edits(text: string) {
  const file = new Project({ useInMemoryFileSystem: true }).createSourceFile(
    '/a.ts',
    text,
  );
  return { file, edits: new TextEdits(file) };
}

describe('TextEdits', () => {
  it('applies every edit against the original offsets', () => {
    const { file, edits: e } = edits('const a = 1;\nconst b = 2;\n');
    const [one, two] = file.getDescendantsOfKind(SyntaxKind.NumericLiteral);
    e.replace(two as never, '20');
    e.replace(one as never, '10');
    expect(e.render()).toBe('const a = 10;\nconst b = 20;\n');
  });

  it('puts insertFirst ahead of other insertions at one offset', () => {
    const { edits: e } = edits('x;\n');
    e.insert(0, 'b');
    e.insertFirst(0, 'a');
    expect(e.render()).toBe('abx;\n');
  });

  it('refuses two edits that overlap', () => {
    const { edits: e } = edits('const a = 1;\n');
    e.replaceRange(0, 8, 'let');
    e.replaceRange(6, 12, 'b = 2');
    expect(() => e.render()).toThrow(OverlappingEditError);
  });

  it('removes a statement with its line and one of the blank lines around it', () => {
    const { file, edits: e } = edits('a();\n\nb();\n\nc();\n');
    e.removeStatement(file.getStatements()[1] as never);
    expect(e.render()).toBe('a();\n\nc();\n');
  });

  it('merges a planned import into an existing import of the specifier', () => {
    const { edits: e } = edits("import { a } from './x';\n\nuse(a);\n");
    e.addImport('./x', 'b');
    e.addImport('./y', 'C', true);
    expect(e.render()).toBe(
      "import { a, b } from './x';\nimport { type C } from './y';\n\nuse(a);\n",
    );
  });

  it('copies a node with the edits inside it applied, and consumes them', () => {
    const { file, edits: e } = edits('f(g(1));\n');
    const inner = file.getDescendantsOfKind(SyntaxKind.CallExpression)[1];
    e.replace(inner?.getArguments()[0] as never, '2');
    const outer = file.getFirstDescendantByKindOrThrow(
      SyntaxKind.CallExpression,
    );
    const copied = e.textOf(outer);
    e.replace(outer, `h(${copied})`);
    expect(e.render()).toBe('h(f(g(2)));\n');
  });
});

describe('indentOf', () => {
  it('reads the indentation of the line a node starts on', () => {
    const { file } = edits('function f() {\n    return 1;\n}\n');
    expect(
      indentOf(
        file.getFirstDescendantByKindOrThrow(SyntaxKind.ReturnStatement),
      ),
    ).toBe('    ');
  });
});

describe('reindent', () => {
  it('moves the lines after the first to a new indentation', () => {
    expect(reindent('f({\n    a: 1,\n  })', '  ', '      ')).toBe(
      'f({\n        a: 1,\n      })',
    );
  });
});
```

- [ ] Step 3: Write the class

`libs/codemod/src/edit/text-edits.ts`:

```ts
import type { Node, SourceFile } from 'ts-morph';

/** One change to a file's text, against the text as it was when the transform started. */
interface Edit {
  readonly start: number;
  readonly end: number;
  readonly text: string;
  readonly order: number;
}

/** A named import a transform needs, added when the edits are applied. */
interface PlannedImport {
  readonly name: string;
  readonly typeOnly: boolean;
}

/** Thrown when two edits of one transform touch the same text: a bug in that transform. */
export class OverlappingEditError extends Error {
  constructor(file: string, at: number) {
    super(`two edits overlap at offset ${at} of ${file}`);
    this.name = 'OverlappingEditError';
  }
}

/**
 * The edits one transform makes to one file, applied in a single pass.
 *
 * Transforms never call ts-morph's manipulation methods. Those re-indent
 * multi-line text by rules of their own and forget every node they touch,
 * so a transform that edits while it reads loses its place. A transform
 * reads the file through ts-morph, records edits against the original
 * offsets here, and the runner applies them all at once.
 */
export class TextEdits {
  private readonly edits: Edit[] = [];
  private readonly imports = new Map<string, PlannedImport[]>();
  private order = 0;
  readonly file: SourceFile;

  constructor(file: SourceFile) {
    this.file = file;
  }

  get size(): number {
    return this.edits.length + this.imports.size;
  }

  replaceRange(start: number, end: number, text: string): void {
    this.edits.push({ start, end, text, order: this.order++ });
  }

  /** Replaces `node`'s text, leaving its leading comments in place. */
  replace(node: Node, text: string): void {
    this.replaceRange(node.getStart(), node.getEnd(), text);
  }

  insert(pos: number, text: string): void {
    this.replaceRange(pos, pos, text);
  }

  /**
   * Inserts ahead of every `insert` at `pos`, keeping the order of
   * `insertFirst` calls: TODO markers go above what a transform wrote there.
   */
  insertFirst(pos: number, text: string): void {
    this.edits.push({
      start: pos,
      end: pos,
      text,
      order: Number.MIN_SAFE_INTEGER + this.order++,
    });
  }

  /**
   * Removes a statement and the lines it spans. When blank lines surround
   * it, one of them goes too, so the removal leaves no double gap.
   */
  removeStatement(node: Node): void {
    const text = this.file.getFullText();
    const lineStart = text.lastIndexOf('\n', node.getStart() - 1) + 1;
    const newline = text.indexOf('\n', node.getEnd());
    const lineEnd = newline === -1 ? text.length : newline + 1;
    const alone =
      text.slice(lineStart, node.getStart()).trim() === '' &&
      text.slice(node.getEnd(), lineEnd).trim() === '';
    if (!alone) {
      this.replaceRange(node.getStart(), node.getEnd(), '');
      return;
    }
    const blankBefore =
      lineStart >= 2 && text.slice(0, lineStart).endsWith('\n\n');
    const blankAfter = text.charAt(lineEnd) === '\n';
    this.replaceRange(
      lineStart,
      blankBefore && blankAfter ? lineEnd + 1 : lineEnd,
      '',
    );
  }

  /** Whether `name` is already planned as an import from `specifier`. */
  plansImport(specifier: string, name: string): boolean {
    return (this.imports.get(specifier) ?? []).some((i) => i.name === name);
  }

  /** Plans `import { name } from 'specifier'`, merged into an existing import of that specifier. */
  addImport(specifier: string, name: string, typeOnly = false): void {
    if (this.plansImport(specifier, name)) return;
    this.imports.set(specifier, [
      ...(this.imports.get(specifier) ?? []),
      { name, typeOnly },
    ]);
  }

  /**
   * `node`'s text with the edits that fall inside it applied, for a
   * transform that copies the node into text it writes. Those edits are
   * consumed, so they are not applied a second time at their old place.
   */
  textOf(node: Node): string {
    const start = node.getStart();
    const end = node.getEnd();
    const inside = this.edits.filter(
      (e) =>
        e.start >= start &&
        e.end <= end &&
        !(e.start === start && e.end === end),
    );
    for (const edit of inside) this.edits.splice(this.edits.indexOf(edit), 1);
    return applyTo(
      node.getText(),
      inside.map((e) => ({ ...e, start: e.start - start, end: e.end - start })),
      this.file.getFilePath(),
    );
  }

  /** Drops every edit inside `node`, for a transform that replaces the whole node. */
  dropWithin(node: Node): void {
    const start = node.getStart();
    const end = node.getEnd();
    for (const edit of this.edits.filter(
      (e) => e.start >= start && e.end <= end,
    )) {
      this.edits.splice(this.edits.indexOf(edit), 1);
    }
  }

  private importEdits(): Edit[] {
    const out: Edit[] = [];
    const declarations = this.file.getImportDeclarations();
    const fresh: string[] = [];
    for (const [specifier, planned] of this.imports) {
      const existing = declarations.find(
        (d) =>
          d.getModuleSpecifierValue() === specifier &&
          !d.getNamespaceImport() &&
          d.getNamedImports().length > 0,
      );
      const names = planned.map((p) =>
        p.typeOnly && !existing?.isTypeOnly() ? `type ${p.name}` : p.name,
      );
      if (existing) {
        const last = existing.getNamedImports().at(-1) as Node;
        out.push({
          start: last.getEnd(),
          end: last.getEnd(),
          text: `, ${names.join(', ')}`,
          order: this.order++,
        });
      } else {
        fresh.push(`import { ${names.join(', ')} } from '${specifier}';`);
      }
    }
    if (fresh.length > 0) {
      const last = declarations.at(-1);
      const pos = last ? last.getEnd() : 0;
      const text = last ? `\n${fresh.join('\n')}` : `${fresh.join('\n')}\n\n`;
      out.push({ start: pos, end: pos, text, order: this.order++ });
    }
    return out;
  }

  /** The file's text with every edit applied. */
  render(): string {
    return applyTo(
      this.file.getFullText(),
      [...this.edits, ...this.importEdits()],
      this.file.getFilePath(),
    );
  }
}

/**
 * Applies edits to `text`. Edits at one offset apply in the order they
 * were made, insertions before a replacement that starts there.
 */
export function applyTo(
  text: string,
  edits: readonly Edit[],
  file: string,
): string {
  const sorted = [...edits].sort(
    (a, b) =>
      a.start - b.start ||
      Number(a.end > a.start) - Number(b.end > b.start) ||
      a.order - b.order,
  );
  let out = '';
  let cursor = 0;
  for (const edit of sorted) {
    if (edit.start < cursor) throw new OverlappingEditError(file, edit.start);
    out += text.slice(cursor, edit.start) + edit.text;
    cursor = edit.end;
  }
  return out + text.slice(cursor);
}

/** The indentation of the line `node` starts on. */
export function indentOf(node: Node): string {
  const text = node.getSourceFile().getFullText();
  const lineStart = text.lastIndexOf('\n', node.getStart() - 1) + 1;
  return /^[ \t]*/.exec(text.slice(lineStart))?.[0] ?? '';
}

/**
 * Moves multi-line `text` copied from a node indented `from` to a place
 * indented `to`: every line after the first keeps its indentation relative
 * to the node.
 */
export function reindent(text: string, from: string, to: string): string {
  return text
    .split('\n')
    .map((line, i) =>
      i === 0 || line.trim() === ''
        ? line
        : `${to}${line.startsWith(from) ? line.slice(from.length) : line.trimStart()}`,
    )
    .join('\n');
}
```

### Task 5: The transform contract, the recorder and the runner

Files:

- Create: `libs/codemod/src/transform.ts`, `libs/codemod/src/recorder.ts`, `libs/codemod/src/runner.ts`, `libs/codemod/src/runner.test.ts`

Interfaces:

- Consumes: `TodoCode`, `NoteCode` (Task 1); `MarkerLostError`, `TransformError`, `UsageError` (Task 2); `anchorOf`, `commentLinesAbove`, `lineStartOf`, `noteMarker`, `oneLine`, `todoMarker`, `findTodoMarkers`, `stripNoteMarkers`, `Position` (Task 3); `TextEdits` (Task 4).
- Produces: `interface MigrationContext<S>` (`project`, `checker`, `state`, `files`, `tsconfigs`, `edits(file)`, `todo(node, code, reason)`, `note(node, code, message)`, `recordChange(file, id)`, `readTextFile(path)`, `writeTextFile(path, text)`, `todoInText(path, code, reason)`); `interface Transform<S>` (`id`, `description`, `runProject?(context)`, `run?(file, context)`); `interface Migration<S>` (`version`, `transforms`, `createState()`); `class Recorder`; `createContext(project, files, tsconfigs, state, recorder)`; `interface LoadedProject { project; files }`; `interface RunOptions<S> { migration; projects; only?; tsconfigs; readTextFile }`; `interface FileResult { path; before; after; transforms }`; `LocatedTodo`, `LocatedNote`; `interface MigrationResult { files; todos; notes }`; `runMigration<S>(options): MigrationResult`.

The runner is transform-major: one transform runs over every file of every project before the next starts, so `module-exports` reads every module as transforms 1 to 5 left it. Nothing touches the disk; the CLI writes the result.

- [ ] Step 1: Write the failing test

`libs/codemod/src/runner.test.ts`:

```ts
import { Project, SyntaxKind } from 'ts-morph';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';

import { MarkerLostError, TransformError, UsageError } from './errors.js';
import { runMigration } from './runner.js';
import type { Migration, Transform } from './transform.js';

function project(files: Record<string, string>) {
  const p = new Project({ useInMemoryFileSystem: true });
  const sources = Object.entries(files).map(([path, text]) =>
    p.createSourceFile(path, text),
  );
  return { project: p, files: sources };
}

function migration(
  ...transforms: Transform<{ seen: string[] }>[]
): Migration<{ seen: string[] }> {
  return { version: '9.9', transforms, createState: () => ({ seen: [] }) };
}

const noText = () => undefined;

describe('runMigration', () => {
  it('runs each transform over every file before the next transform starts', () => {
    const order: string[] = [];
    const record = (id: string): Transform<{ seen: string[] }> => ({
      id,
      description: id,
      run: (file) => void order.push(`${id}:${file.getBaseName()}`),
    });
    runMigration({
      migration: migration(record('one'), record('two')),
      projects: [project({ '/a.ts': '', '/b.ts': '' })],
      tsconfigs: [],
      readTextFile: noText,
    });
    expect(order).toEqual(['one:a.ts', 'one:b.ts', 'two:a.ts', 'two:b.ts']);
  });

  it('returns each changed file with the transforms that changed it, and the located TODOs and notes', () => {
    const rename: Transform<{ seen: string[] }> = {
      id: 'rename',
      description: 'renames a to b',
      run(file, context) {
        const id = file
          .getDescendantsOfKind(SyntaxKind.Identifier)
          .find((i) => i.getText() === 'a');
        if (!id) return;
        context.edits(file).replace(id, 'b');
        context.todo(id, 'container-list', 'check b');
        context.note(id, 'undecorated-param-injected', 'b is injected');
      },
    };
    const result = runMigration({
      migration: migration(rename),
      projects: [project({ '/a.ts': 'function f() {\n  const a = 1;\n}\n' })],
      tsconfigs: [],
      readTextFile: noText,
    });
    expect(result.files).toEqual([
      {
        path: '/a.ts',
        before: 'function f() {\n  const a = 1;\n}\n',
        after:
          'function f() {\n  // TODO(nexusdi-0.4): check b (container-list)\n  const b = 1;\n}\n',
        transforms: ['rename'],
      },
    ]);
    expect(result.todos).toEqual([
      {
        path: '/a.ts',
        code: 'container-list',
        reason: 'check b',
        line: 3,
        column: 3,
      },
    ]);
    expect(result.notes).toEqual([
      {
        path: '/a.ts',
        code: 'undecorated-param-injected',
        message: 'b is injected',
        line: 3,
        column: 9,
      },
    ]);
  });

  it('runs only the transforms --only names, in migration order', () => {
    const ran: string[] = [];
    const t = (id: string): Transform<{ seen: string[] }> => ({
      id,
      description: id,
      run: () => void ran.push(id),
    });
    runMigration({
      migration: migration(t('a'), t('b'), t('c')),
      projects: [project({ '/x.ts': '' })],
      tsconfigs: [],
      only: ['c', 'a'],
      readTextFile: noText,
    });
    expect(ran).toEqual(['a', 'c']);
  });

  it('refuses an --only id the migration does not have', () => {
    const run = () =>
      runMigration({
        migration: migration(),
        projects: [],
        tsconfigs: [],
        only: ['nope'],
        readTextFile: noText,
      });
    expect(run).toThrow(UsageError);
  });

  it('wraps a throwing transform with its id and file', () => {
    const broken: Transform<{ seen: string[] }> = {
      id: 'broken',
      description: '',
      run: () => {
        throw new Error('boom');
      },
    };
    const run = () =>
      runMigration({
        migration: migration(broken),
        projects: [project({ '/a.ts': '' })],
        tsconfigs: [],
        readTextFile: noText,
      });
    expect(run).toThrow(TransformError);
    expect(run).toThrow('transform broken failed on /a.ts: boom');
  });

  it('fails when a later transform removes the marker an earlier one wrote', () => {
    const mark: Transform<{ seen: string[] }> = {
      id: 'mark',
      description: '',
      run: (file, context) =>
        context.todo(file.getStatements()[0] as never, 'container-list', 'x'),
    };
    const wipe: Transform<{ seen: string[] }> = {
      id: 'wipe',
      description: '',
      run: (file, context) =>
        context.edits(file).replaceRange(0, file.getFullText().length, 'y;\n'),
    };
    const run = () =>
      runMigration({
        migration: migration(mark, wipe),
        projects: [project({ '/a.ts': 'x;\n' })],
        tsconfigs: [],
        readTextFile: noText,
      });
    expect(run).toThrow(MarkerLostError);
  });

  it('shares one state object between the transforms of a run', () => {
    const write: Transform<{ seen: string[] }> = {
      id: 'w',
      description: '',
      run: (file, c) => void c.state.seen.push(file.getBaseName()),
    };
    let read: string[] = [];
    const check: Transform<{ seen: string[] }> = {
      id: 'r',
      description: '',
      runProject: (c) => void (read = [...c.state.seen]),
    };
    runMigration({
      migration: migration(write, check),
      projects: [project({ '/a.ts': '' })],
      tsconfigs: [],
      readTextFile: noText,
    });
    expect(read).toEqual(['a.ts']);
  });
});
```

- [ ] Step 3: Write the transform contract

`libs/codemod/src/transform.ts`:

```ts
import type { Node, Project, SourceFile, TypeChecker } from 'ts-morph';

import type { NoteCode, TodoCode } from './codes.js';
import type { TextEdits } from './edit/text-edits.js';

/**
 * What a transform receives besides the file it rewrites.
 *
 * One context exists per loaded ts-morph project. `state` is shared by every
 * context of one run, so a monorepo with several tsconfig leaves still has
 * one module graph.
 */
export interface MigrationContext<S = unknown> {
  readonly project: Project;
  readonly checker: TypeChecker;
  readonly state: S;
  /** The files this run rewrites, across every loaded project. */
  readonly files: readonly SourceFile[];
  /** Every tsconfig the run loaded: the root and every config it references. */
  readonly tsconfigs: readonly string[];
  /**
   * The pending edits of `file`. The runner applies them when the transform
   * has finished the file, against the text the transform read.
   */
  edits(file: SourceFile): TextEdits;
  /**
   * Marks `node`'s statement with `// TODO(nexusdi-0.4): <reason> (<code>)`
   * and adds a report entry. Nothing happens when the statement already
   * carries that exact marker, which keeps a second run from adding one.
   */
  todo(node: Node, code: TodoCode, reason: string): void;
  /** Adds a report entry at `node` and changes no code. */
  note(node: Node, code: NoteCode, message: string): void;
  /** Records that `transformId` changed `file`. The runner calls it. */
  recordChange(file: SourceFile, transformId: string): void;
  /** Reads a non-source file, with any edit an earlier transform staged. */
  readTextFile(path: string): string | undefined;
  /** Stages new content for a non-source file, such as a tsconfig. */
  writeTextFile(path: string, text: string): void;
  /**
   * Records a TODO whose marker line the transform wrote into text itself:
   * into generated code, or into a staged text file.
   */
  todoInText(path: string, code: TodoCode, reason: string): void;
}

/** One step of a migration. */
export interface Transform<S = unknown> {
  /** Stable id: the report and --only use it. */
  readonly id: string;
  readonly description: string;
  /** Runs once per loaded project, before `run` sees that project's files. */
  runProject?(context: MigrationContext<S>): void;
  /** Rewrites one source file. */
  run?(file: SourceFile, context: MigrationContext<S>): void;
}

/** A migration: its transforms, in order, and the state they share. */
export interface Migration<S = unknown> {
  readonly version: string;
  readonly transforms: readonly Transform<S>[];
  createState(): S;
}
```

- [ ] Step 4: Write the recorder

`libs/codemod/src/recorder.ts`:

```ts
import { Node, type Project, type SourceFile } from 'ts-morph';

import type { NoteCode, TodoCode } from './codes.js';
import { TextEdits } from './edit/text-edits.js';
import {
  anchorOf,
  commentLinesAbove,
  lineStartOf,
  noteMarker,
  oneLine,
  todoMarker,
} from './markers.js';
import type { MigrationContext } from './transform.js';

/** A TODO the run wrote, before its position is known. */
export interface TodoEntry {
  readonly path: string;
  readonly code: TodoCode;
  readonly reason: string;
}

/** A note the run recorded, keyed by the marker id it left in the text. */
export interface NoteEntry {
  readonly path: string;
  readonly id: number;
  readonly code: NoteCode;
  readonly message: string;
}

/** A non-source file the run edits, such as a tsconfig. */
export interface TextFileEdit {
  readonly before: string;
  after: string;
}

/**
 * Collects what transforms record for each file and applies it in one pass.
 *
 * A transform's edits, TODOs and notes stay pending until it has finished
 * the file. `flush` then writes them all against the text the transform
 * read, so no offset a transform computed goes stale.
 */
export class Recorder {
  readonly todos: TodoEntry[] = [];
  readonly notes: NoteEntry[] = [];
  /** Transform ids that changed each file, by path, in run order. */
  readonly changes = new Map<string, string[]>();
  readonly textFiles = new Map<string, TextFileEdit>();
  private readonly edits = new Map<SourceFile, TextEdits>();
  private readonly pending = new Map<
    SourceFile,
    { todos: TodoEntry[]; markers: { pos: number; text: string }[] }
  >();
  private nextNoteId = 0;
  private readonly readText: (path: string) => string | undefined;

  constructor(readText: (path: string) => string | undefined) {
    this.readText = readText;
  }

  editsOf(file: SourceFile): TextEdits {
    let edits = this.edits.get(file);
    if (!edits) {
      edits = new TextEdits(file);
      this.edits.set(file, edits);
    }
    return edits;
  }

  private pendingOf(file: SourceFile) {
    let found = this.pending.get(file);
    if (!found) {
      found = { todos: [], markers: [] };
      this.pending.set(file, found);
    }
    return found;
  }

  todo(node: Node, code: TodoCode, reason: string): void {
    const file = node.getSourceFile();
    const text = file.getFullText();
    const pos = anchorOf(node).getStart(true);
    const marker = todoMarker(code, reason);
    const pending = this.pendingOf(file);
    // A later transform can move marked code under a new statement, so every enclosing line is checked.
    for (
      let current: Node | undefined = node;
      current && !Node.isSourceFile(current);
      current = current.getParent()
    ) {
      if (commentLinesAbove(text, current.getStart(true)).includes(marker))
        return;
    }
    if (pending.markers.some((m) => m.pos === pos && m.text.includes(marker)))
      return;
    const lineStart = lineStartOf(text, pos);
    const before = text.slice(lineStart, pos);
    const indent = /^[ \t]*/.exec(before)?.[0] ?? '';
    const atLineStart = before.trim() === '';
    pending.markers.push({
      pos,
      text: atLineStart
        ? `${marker}\n${indent}`
        : `\n${indent}${marker}\n${indent}`,
    });
    pending.todos.push({
      path: file.getFilePath(),
      code,
      reason: oneLine(reason),
    });
  }

  note(node: Node, code: NoteCode, message: string): void {
    const file = node.getSourceFile();
    const id = this.nextNoteId++;
    this.notes.push({
      path: file.getFilePath(),
      id,
      code,
      message: oneLine(message),
    });
    this.pendingOf(file).markers.push({
      pos: node.getStart(),
      text: noteMarker(id),
    });
  }

  /** Records a TODO whose marker a transform wrote into text itself. */
  todoInText(path: string, code: TodoCode, reason: string): void {
    this.todos.push({ path, code, reason: oneLine(reason) });
  }

  recordChange(path: string, transformId: string): void {
    const list = this.changes.get(path) ?? [];
    if (!list.includes(transformId)) list.push(transformId);
    this.changes.set(path, list);
  }

  readTextFile(path: string): string | undefined {
    return this.textFiles.get(path)?.after ?? this.readText(path);
  }

  writeTextFile(path: string, text: string): void {
    const existing = this.textFiles.get(path);
    if (existing) {
      existing.after = text;
      return;
    }
    const before = this.readText(path);
    if (before === undefined)
      throw new Error(`cannot stage ${path}: it does not exist`);
    this.textFiles.set(path, { before, after: text });
  }

  /** Applies every pending edit, marker and note of `file` in one pass. */
  flush(file: SourceFile): void {
    const edits = this.edits.get(file) ?? new TextEdits(file);
    const pending = this.pending.get(file);
    this.edits.delete(file);
    this.pending.delete(file);
    for (const marker of pending?.markers ?? [])
      edits.insertFirst(marker.pos, marker.text);
    this.todos.push(...(pending?.todos ?? []));
    if (edits.size === 0) return;
    const out = edits.render();
    if (out !== file.getFullText()) file.replaceWithText(out);
  }
}

/** The context one project's transforms receive. */
export function createContext<S>(
  project: Project,
  files: readonly SourceFile[],
  tsconfigs: readonly string[],
  state: S,
  recorder: Recorder,
): MigrationContext<S> {
  return {
    project,
    tsconfigs,
    get checker() {
      return project.getTypeChecker();
    },
    state,
    files,
    edits: (file) => recorder.editsOf(file),
    todo: (node, code, reason) => recorder.todo(node, code, reason),
    note: (node, code, message) => recorder.note(node, code, message),
    recordChange: (file, transformId) =>
      recorder.recordChange(file.getFilePath(), transformId),
    readTextFile: (path) => recorder.readTextFile(path),
    writeTextFile: (path, text) => recorder.writeTextFile(path, text),
    todoInText: (path, code, reason) => recorder.todoInText(path, code, reason),
  };
}
```

- [ ] Step 5: Write the runner

`libs/codemod/src/runner.ts`:

```ts
import type { Project, SourceFile } from 'ts-morph';

import type { NoteCode, TodoCode } from './codes.js';
import { MarkerLostError, TransformError, UsageError } from './errors.js';
import {
  findTodoMarkers,
  type Position,
  stripNoteMarkers,
  todoMarker,
} from './markers.js';
import { createContext, Recorder } from './recorder.js';
import type { Migration, MigrationContext } from './transform.js';

/** A ts-morph project and the files of it this run rewrites. */
export interface LoadedProject {
  readonly project: Project;
  readonly files: readonly SourceFile[];
}

export interface RunOptions<S> {
  readonly migration: Migration<S>;
  readonly projects: readonly LoadedProject[];
  /** Transform ids to run. Every transform when absent. */
  readonly only?: readonly string[];
  /** Every tsconfig the run loaded. */
  readonly tsconfigs: readonly string[];
  /** Reads a non-source file from disk, such as a tsconfig. */
  readonly readTextFile: (path: string) => string | undefined;
}

export interface FileResult {
  readonly path: string;
  readonly before: string;
  readonly after: string;
  /** The transforms that changed the file, in run order. */
  readonly transforms: readonly string[];
}

export interface LocatedTodo extends Position {
  readonly path: string;
  readonly code: TodoCode;
  readonly reason: string;
}

export interface LocatedNote extends Position {
  readonly path: string;
  readonly code: NoteCode;
  readonly message: string;
}

export interface MigrationResult {
  /** Every file the run changed, sorted by path. */
  readonly files: readonly FileResult[];
  readonly todos: readonly LocatedTodo[];
  readonly notes: readonly LocatedNote[];
}

function byPosition(
  a: Position & { path: string },
  b: Position & { path: string },
): number {
  return a.path.localeCompare(b.path) || a.line - b.line || a.column - b.column;
}

/**
 * Runs a migration over loaded projects and returns what it would write.
 *
 * Transforms run one at a time over every file of every project, so a
 * transform reads the output of every transform before it in every file.
 * Nothing touches the disk: the caller writes the result or prints it.
 */
export function runMigration<S>(options: RunOptions<S>): MigrationResult {
  const { migration, projects } = options;
  const known = new Set(migration.transforms.map((t) => t.id));
  const unknown = (options.only ?? []).filter((id) => !known.has(id));
  if (unknown.length > 0) {
    throw new UsageError(
      `unknown transform ${unknown.join(', ')}; migration ${migration.version} has ${[...known].join(', ')}`,
    );
  }
  const transforms = options.only
    ? migration.transforms.filter((t) => options.only?.includes(t.id))
    : migration.transforms;

  const allFiles = projects.flatMap((p) => p.files);
  const originals = new Map(
    allFiles.map((f) => [f.getFilePath(), f.getFullText()]),
  );
  const state = migration.createState();
  const recorder = new Recorder(options.readTextFile);
  const contexts: [LoadedProject, MigrationContext<S>][] = projects.map((p) => [
    p,
    createContext(p.project, allFiles, options.tsconfigs, state, recorder),
  ]);

  for (const transform of transforms) {
    for (const [loaded, context] of contexts) {
      if (transform.runProject) {
        const snapshot = new Map(loaded.files.map((f) => [f, f.getFullText()]));
        const textSnapshot = new Map(
          [...recorder.textFiles].map(([path, edit]) => [path, edit.after]),
        );
        try {
          transform.runProject(context);
        } catch (error) {
          const where = String(
            loaded.project.getCompilerOptions()['configFilePath'] ??
              '(project)',
          );
          throw new TransformError(transform.id, where, error);
        }
        for (const file of loaded.files) {
          recorder.flush(file);
          if (file.getFullText() !== snapshot.get(file))
            context.recordChange(file, transform.id);
        }
        for (const [path, edit] of recorder.textFiles) {
          if (textSnapshot.get(path) !== edit.after)
            recorder.recordChange(path, transform.id);
        }
      }
      if (!transform.run) continue;
      for (const file of loaded.files) {
        const before = file.getFullText();
        try {
          transform.run(file, context);
          recorder.flush(file);
        } catch (error) {
          throw new TransformError(transform.id, file.getFilePath(), error);
        }
        if (file.getFullText() !== before)
          context.recordChange(file, transform.id);
      }
    }
  }

  return finalize(allFiles, originals, recorder);
}

function finalize(
  files: readonly SourceFile[],
  originals: Map<string, string>,
  recorder: Recorder,
): MigrationResult {
  const notePositions = new Map<number, Position>();
  const finalText = new Map<string, string>();

  for (const file of files) {
    const { text, positions } = stripNoteMarkers(file.getFullText());
    if (text !== file.getFullText()) file.replaceWithText(text);
    for (const [id, position] of positions) notePositions.set(id, position);
    finalText.set(file.getFilePath(), text);
  }
  for (const [path, edit] of recorder.textFiles)
    finalText.set(path, edit.after);

  const todos: LocatedTodo[] = [];
  const claimed = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const entry of recorder.todos) {
    const text = finalText.get(entry.path) ?? '';
    const marker = todoMarker(entry.code, entry.reason);
    const key = `${entry.path}\0${marker}`;
    const index = claimed.get(key) ?? 0;
    const position = findTodoMarkers(text, marker)[index];
    if (!position) throw new MarkerLostError(entry.path, entry.code);
    claimed.set(key, index + 1);
    todos.push({ ...entry, ...position });
  }

  const notes: LocatedNote[] = recorder.notes.map((entry) => {
    const position = notePositions.get(entry.id);
    if (!position) throw new MarkerLostError(entry.path, entry.code);
    return {
      path: entry.path,
      code: entry.code,
      message: entry.message,
      ...position,
    };
  });

  const results: FileResult[] = [];
  for (const file of files) {
    const path = file.getFilePath();
    const before = originals.get(path) ?? '';
    const after = file.getFullText();
    if (after !== before)
      results.push({
        path,
        before,
        after,
        transforms: recorder.changes.get(path) ?? [],
      });
  }
  for (const [path, edit] of recorder.textFiles) {
    if (edit.after !== edit.before) {
      results.push({
        path,
        before: edit.before,
        after: edit.after,
        transforms: recorder.changes.get(path) ?? [],
      });
    }
  }
  results.sort((a, b) => a.path.localeCompare(b.path));

  return {
    files: results,
    todos: todos.sort(byPosition),
    notes: notes.sort(byPosition),
  };
}
```

### Task 6: Load projects from a tsconfig, its references and the given paths

Files:

- Create: `libs/codemod/src/project/load-projects.ts`, `libs/codemod/src/project/load-projects.test.ts`

Interfaces:

- Consumes: `ProjectLoadError`, `UsageError` (Task 2); `LoadedProject` (Task 5).
- Produces: `interface LoadOptions { tsconfig: string; paths?: string[]; fileSystem?: FileSystemHost }`; `interface LoadedWorkspace { projects: LoadedProject[]; tsconfigs: string[]; warnings: string[] }`; `loadProjects(options): LoadedWorkspace`.

A monorepo root is usually a solution config (`"files": []` plus `references`), which compiles nothing itself, so the loader builds one ts-morph project per referenced config that compiles files. One ts-morph project has one set of compiler options, and a React app's `tsconfig.app.json` and `tsconfig.spec.json` differ in `jsx`, `types` and `module`. A reference outside the root config's directory is not followed: an app's reference to a library it builds against is not a request to migrate that library.

- [ ] Step 1: Write the failing test

`libs/codemod/src/project/load-projects.test.ts`:

```ts
import { InMemoryFileSystemHost } from 'ts-morph';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';

import { ProjectLoadError, UsageError } from '../errors.js';
import { loadProjects } from './load-projects.js';

function host(files: Record<string, string>): InMemoryFileSystemHost {
  const fs = new InMemoryFileSystemHost();
  for (const [path, text] of Object.entries(files))
    fs.writeFileSync(path, text);
  return fs;
}

const paths = (workspace: ReturnType<typeof loadProjects>) =>
  workspace.projects.map((p) => p.files.map((f) => f.getFilePath()));

describe('loadProjects', () => {
  it('loads each referenced config that compiles files, and skips a solution config', () => {
    const fileSystem = host({
      '/app/tsconfig.json':
        '{ "files": [], "include": [], "references": [{ "path": "./tsconfig.app.json" }, { "path": "./lib" }] }',
      '/app/tsconfig.app.json': '{ "include": ["src"] }',
      '/app/lib/tsconfig.json': '{ "include": ["**/*.ts"] }',
      '/app/src/a.ts': 'export const a = 1;',
      '/app/lib/b.ts': 'export const b = 1;',
    });
    const workspace = loadProjects({
      tsconfig: '/app/tsconfig.json',
      fileSystem,
    });
    expect(paths(workspace)).toEqual([['/app/src/a.ts'], ['/app/lib/b.ts']]);
    expect(workspace.tsconfigs).toEqual([
      '/app/tsconfig.json',
      '/app/tsconfig.app.json',
      '/app/lib/tsconfig.json',
    ]);
  });

  it('migrates a file two configs include once', () => {
    const fileSystem = host({
      '/app/tsconfig.json':
        '{ "files": [], "references": [{ "path": "./one.json" }, { "path": "./two.json" }] }',
      '/app/one.json': '{ "include": ["src"] }',
      '/app/two.json': '{ "include": ["src"] }',
      '/app/src/a.ts': '',
    });
    expect(
      paths(loadProjects({ tsconfig: '/app/tsconfig.json', fileSystem })),
    ).toEqual([['/app/src/a.ts'], []]);
  });

  it('does not follow a reference outside the root config directory, and says so', () => {
    const fileSystem = host({
      '/repo/app/tsconfig.json':
        '{ "include": ["src"], "references": [{ "path": "../lib" }] }',
      '/repo/lib/tsconfig.json': '{ "include": ["src"] }',
      '/repo/app/src/a.ts': '',
    });
    const workspace = loadProjects({
      tsconfig: '/repo/app/tsconfig.json',
      fileSystem,
    });
    expect(workspace.tsconfigs).toEqual(['/repo/app/tsconfig.json']);
    expect(workspace.warnings).toEqual([
      '/repo/app/tsconfig.json references /repo/lib/tsconfig.json, outside /repo/app; not migrated',
    ]);
  });

  it('restricts the run to the given paths, and adds a named file no config includes', () => {
    const fileSystem = host({
      '/app/tsconfig.json': '{ "include": ["src"] }',
      '/app/src/a.ts': '',
      '/app/src/b.ts': '',
      '/app/tests/a.test.ts': '',
    });
    const workspace = loadProjects({
      tsconfig: '/app/tsconfig.json',
      fileSystem,
      paths: ['/app/src/a.ts', '/app/tests'],
    });
    expect(paths(workspace)).toEqual([
      ['/app/src/a.ts', '/app/tests/a.test.ts'],
    ]);
  });

  it('refuses a path that matches no TypeScript file', () => {
    const fileSystem = host({
      '/app/tsconfig.json': '{ "include": ["src"] }',
      '/app/src/a.ts': '',
    });
    expect(() =>
      loadProjects({
        tsconfig: '/app/tsconfig.json',
        fileSystem,
        paths: ['/app/nowhere'],
      }),
    ).toThrow(UsageError);
  });

  it('fails on a missing or unparsable tsconfig', () => {
    expect(() =>
      loadProjects({ tsconfig: '/none/tsconfig.json', fileSystem: host({}) }),
    ).toThrow(ProjectLoadError);
    expect(() =>
      loadProjects({
        tsconfig: '/app/tsconfig.json',
        fileSystem: host({ '/app/tsconfig.json': '{ "include": [' }),
      }),
    ).toThrow(ProjectLoadError);
  });

  it('loads a config that uses an option TypeScript 6 deprecates', () => {
    const fileSystem = host({
      '/app/tsconfig.json':
        '{ "compilerOptions": { "moduleResolution": "node", "baseUrl": "." }, "include": ["src"] }',
      '/app/src/a.ts': '',
    });
    expect(
      paths(loadProjects({ tsconfig: '/app/tsconfig.json', fileSystem })),
    ).toEqual([['/app/src/a.ts']]);
  });
});
```

- [ ] Step 3: Write the loader

`libs/codemod/src/project/load-projects.ts`:

```ts
import { dirname, join, resolve } from 'node:path';

import { type ParseError, parse, printParseErrorCode } from 'jsonc-parser';
import { type FileSystemHost, Project, type SourceFile } from 'ts-morph';

import { ProjectLoadError, UsageError } from '../errors.js';
import type { LoadedProject } from '../runner.js';

export interface LoadOptions {
  /** Absolute path of the tsconfig to start from. */
  readonly tsconfig: string;
  /** Absolute file or directory paths to restrict the run to. Every project file when empty. */
  readonly paths?: readonly string[];
  /** The file system to read. The real one when absent. */
  readonly fileSystem?: FileSystemHost;
}

export interface LoadedWorkspace {
  readonly projects: readonly LoadedProject[];
  /** Every tsconfig the run loaded: the root and every config it references. */
  readonly tsconfigs: readonly string[];
  /** References the load skipped, for the CLI to print. */
  readonly warnings: readonly string[];
}

const SOURCE = /\.(?:[cm]?ts|tsx)$/;

interface ConfigJson {
  readonly files?: readonly string[];
  readonly include?: readonly string[];
  readonly references?: readonly { readonly path: string }[];
}

function readConfig(fs: FileSystemHost, path: string): ConfigJson {
  if (!fs.fileExistsSync(path))
    throw new ProjectLoadError(path, 'the file does not exist');
  const errors: ParseError[] = [];
  const json = parse(fs.readFileSync(path), errors, {
    allowTrailingComma: true,
  }) as unknown;
  if (errors.length > 0) {
    const first = errors[0];
    throw new ProjectLoadError(
      path,
      `${printParseErrorCode(first?.error ?? 0)} at offset ${first?.offset}`,
    );
  }
  if (typeof json !== 'object' || json === null)
    throw new ProjectLoadError(path, 'the file is not a JSON object');
  return json as ConfigJson;
}

function referencePath(fs: FileSystemHost, from: string, ref: string): string {
  const target = resolve(dirname(from), ref);
  return target.endsWith('.json') && !fs.directoryExistsSync(target)
    ? target
    : join(target, 'tsconfig.json');
}

/**
 * The configs reachable from `root` through `references`, in walk order,
 * and which of them compile files of their own. A solution config with
 * `"files": []` and only references compiles nothing.
 *
 * A reference outside the root config's directory is not followed: it
 * points at a dependency the user did not ask to migrate, such as an app's
 * reference to a library it builds against. A reference to a missing
 * config is skipped, as tsc reports it and carries on.
 */
function walkReferences(
  fs: FileSystemHost,
  root: string,
): { all: string[]; leaves: string[]; warnings: string[] } {
  const all: string[] = [];
  const leaves: string[] = [];
  const warnings: string[] = [];
  const scope = dirname(root);
  const visit = (path: string): void => {
    if (all.includes(path)) return;
    all.push(path);
    const config = readConfig(fs, path);
    const references = config.references ?? [];
    const empty = (list: readonly string[] | undefined) =>
      list === undefined || list.length === 0;
    const solutionOnly =
      references.length > 0 &&
      Array.isArray(config.files) &&
      config.files.length === 0 &&
      empty(config.include);
    if (!solutionOnly) leaves.push(path);
    for (const ref of references) {
      const target = referencePath(fs, path, ref.path);
      if (!within(target, scope))
        warnings.push(
          `${path} references ${target}, outside ${scope}; not migrated`,
        );
      else if (!fs.fileExistsSync(target))
        warnings.push(
          `${path} references ${target}, which does not exist; skipped`,
        );
      else visit(target);
    }
  };
  visit(root);
  return { all, leaves, warnings };
}

function isMigratable(file: SourceFile): boolean {
  return (
    !file.isDeclarationFile() &&
    !file.isInNodeModules() &&
    SOURCE.test(file.getFilePath())
  );
}

function within(path: string, filter: string): boolean {
  return path === filter || path.startsWith(`${filter.replace(/\/$/, '')}/`);
}

/**
 * Loads the tsconfig at `options.tsconfig` and every config it references
 * as ts-morph projects, one per config that compiles files.
 *
 * A file two configs include is migrated once, by the first config that
 * includes it. A path in `options.paths` that no config includes is added
 * to the first project, because an app's tests often sit outside every
 * tsconfig. Config diagnostics do not stop the load: TypeScript 6 reports
 * options it deprecates as errors, and the codemod needs types, not a
 * clean compile, so `ignoreDeprecations` is set for the run.
 */
export function loadProjects(options: LoadOptions): LoadedWorkspace {
  const fileSystem = options.fileSystem;
  const probe = fileSystem ?? new Project().getFileSystem();
  const { all, leaves, warnings } = walkReferences(probe, options.tsconfig);

  const projects = leaves.map(
    (tsConfigFilePath) =>
      new Project({
        tsConfigFilePath,
        ...(fileSystem ? { fileSystem } : {}),
        compilerOptions: { ignoreDeprecations: '6.0', noEmit: true },
      }),
  );
  if (projects.length === 0)
    throw new ProjectLoadError(
      options.tsconfig,
      'no config in it compiles files',
    );

  const filters = options.paths ?? [];
  const claimed = new Set<string>();
  const selected = projects.map((project) =>
    project.getSourceFiles().filter((file) => {
      const path = file.getFilePath();
      if (!isMigratable(file) || claimed.has(path)) return false;
      if (filters.length > 0 && !filters.some((f) => within(path, f)))
        return false;
      claimed.add(path);
      return true;
    }),
  );

  const first = projects[0] as Project;
  for (const filter of filters) {
    if ([...claimed].some((path) => within(path, filter))) continue;
    const fs = first.getFileSystem();
    const added = fs.directoryExistsSync(filter)
      ? first.addSourceFilesAtPaths([
          `${filter}/**/*.{ts,tsx,mts,cts}`,
          `!${filter}/**/node_modules/**`,
          `!${filter}/**/*.d.ts`,
        ])
      : fs.fileExistsSync(filter) && SOURCE.test(filter)
        ? [first.addSourceFileAtPath(filter)]
        : [];
    if (added.length === 0)
      throw new UsageError(`${filter} matches no TypeScript file`);
    for (const file of added) {
      if (claimed.has(file.getFilePath())) continue;
      claimed.add(file.getFilePath());
      selected[0]?.push(file);
    }
  }

  return {
    projects: projects.map((project, i) => ({
      project,
      files: selected[i] ?? [],
    })),
    tsconfigs: all,
    warnings,
  };
}
```

### Task 7: Recognise the 0.3 API by its imports

Files:

- Create: `libs/codemod/src/core-api.ts`, `libs/codemod/src/core-api.test.ts`

Interfaces:

- Consumes: nothing from earlier tasks.
- Produces: `CORE = '@nexusdi/core'`; `coreExportName(node): string | undefined`; `isCoreExport(node, ...names): boolean`; `coreCallName(node: Decorator | CallExpression | NewExpression): string | undefined` (the default import reads as `'Nexus'`); `declarationOf(expr): Node | undefined`; `findDeclaration(files, key): Node | undefined`; `declarationKey(node): string` (`file#name`); `class CoreTypes` with `isContainer(expr)` and `isToken(expr)`; `coreTypesOf(project): CoreTypes`.

Detection reads imports, because the declarations an import resolves to depend on what the user has installed: `@nexusdi/core` 0.3.1 during a normal migration, the 0.4 workspace source in the end-to-end test, and nothing in a snippet. Keys are `file#name` strings, because ts-morph hands out new symbol objects each time it rebuilds the program after an edit.

- [ ] Step 1: Write the failing test

`libs/codemod/src/core-api.test.ts`:

```ts
import { Project, SyntaxKind } from 'ts-morph';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';

import {
  coreCallName,
  coreExportName,
  coreTypesOf,
  declarationKey,
  findDeclaration,
} from './core-api.js';

function files(sources: Record<string, string>) {
  const project = new Project({
    useInMemoryFileSystem: true,
    compilerOptions: { experimentalDecorators: true },
  });
  const created = Object.entries(sources).map(([path, text]) =>
    project.createSourceFile(path, text),
  );
  return { project, created };
}

const identifier = (
  file: ReturnType<typeof files>['created'][number],
  text: string,
) =>
  file
    .getDescendantsOfKind(SyntaxKind.Identifier)
    .filter((i) => i.getText() === text)
    .at(-1) as never;

describe('coreExportName', () => {
  it('names the export an import binds, through an alias', () => {
    const { created } = files({
      '/a.ts': "import { Service as Svc } from '@nexusdi/core';\nSvc;\n",
    });
    expect(coreExportName(identifier(created[0] as never, 'Svc'))).toBe(
      'Service',
    );
  });

  it('follows a re-export through a barrel file of the project', () => {
    const { created } = files({
      '/di.ts': "export { Inject } from '@nexusdi/core';\n",
      '/a.ts': "import { Inject } from './di';\nInject;\n",
    });
    expect(coreExportName(identifier(created[1] as never, 'Inject'))).toBe(
      'Inject',
    );
  });

  it('names a member of a namespace import', () => {
    const { created } = files({
      '/a.ts': "import * as core from '@nexusdi/core';\ncore.Module;\n",
    });
    const access = created[0]?.getFirstDescendantByKindOrThrow(
      SyntaxKind.PropertyAccessExpression,
    );
    expect(coreExportName(access as never)).toBe('Module');
  });

  it('ignores a local binding that shadows the name', () => {
    const { created } = files({ '/a.ts': 'function Service() {}\nService;\n' });
    expect(
      coreExportName(identifier(created[0] as never, 'Service')),
    ).toBeUndefined();
  });
});

describe('coreCallName', () => {
  it('reports the default import as Nexus', () => {
    const { created } = files({
      '/a.ts': "import Container from '@nexusdi/core';\nnew Container();\n",
    });
    expect(
      coreCallName(
        created[0]?.getFirstDescendantByKindOrThrow(
          SyntaxKind.NewExpression,
        ) as never,
      ),
    ).toBe('Nexus');
  });
});

describe('CoreTypes', () => {
  it('recognises a container bound to new Nexus() or to await Nexus.create()', () => {
    const { project, created } = files({
      '/a.ts':
        "import { Nexus } from '@nexusdi/core';\nconst a = new Nexus();\nasync function f() { const b = await Nexus.create(R); b; }\na;\n",
    });
    const types = coreTypesOf(project);
    expect(types.isContainer(identifier(created[0] as never, 'a'))).toBe(true);
    expect(types.isContainer(identifier(created[0] as never, 'b'))).toBe(true);
  });
});

describe('findDeclaration', () => {
  it('finds a declaration again by the key declarationKey gave it', () => {
    const { created } = files({ '/a.ts': 'export const TOKEN = 1;\n' });
    const declaration = created[0]?.getVariableDeclarationOrThrow('TOKEN');
    expect(findDeclaration(created, declarationKey(declaration as never))).toBe(
      declaration,
    );
  });
});
```
